## 2,752. judge:sequence-mcq-expansion-v2-01146

**Question type:** protein_function

<table>
<thead><tr><th>Original question</th><th>Skill-informed rewrite</th></tr></thead>
<tbody><tr>
<td valign="top"><strong>Question:</strong> Which option best describes the biological function of this protein?<br><br><strong>Choices:</strong><br><br><strong>A.</strong> An active kinase that promotes CRK-II signaling and supports membrane ruffling.<br><br><strong>B.</strong> A probable catalytically inactive kinase that interacts with CRK-II and antagonizes its signaling, preventing CRK-II-dependent membrane ruffling and lamellipodia-like extensions.<br><br><strong>C.</strong> A scaffold that activates CRK-II signaling to promote membrane ruffling and lamellipodia-like extensions.<br><br><strong>D.</strong> A small-GTPase regulator that directly drives lamellipodia formation independently of CRK-II.<br><br><strong>Flash-Next response:</strong><br><br><br><br>1. reasoning: Analyzing the protein sequence reveals several key features:<br><br>**Kinase-like domain characteristics:** The sequence contains regions resembling a kinase domain (e.g., the segment around &quot;RLLLTDFG&quot; resembles a DFG-like motif region), but critically, it lacks the canonical catalytic residues required for kinase activity (the VAIK lysine, HRD aspartate, and DFG motif in their proper positions are either absent or degenerate). This is a hallmark of a **pseudokinase** — a protein with kinase-like structural features but no catalytic activity.<br><br>**Proline-rich regions:** The sequence contains multiple stretches rich in proline residues (e.g., &quot;PPPEPPEPDNPTWSTQPTYSN,&quot; &quot;PPPLPKKILTRTQSLPTRRT,&quot; etc.). Proline-rich regions are known to interact with SH3 domains, which are present in adaptor proteins like CRK-II.<br><br>**Functional interpretation:** A pseudokinase with proline-rich SH3-binding motifs would interact with CRK-II but, being catalytically inactive, would not propagate signaling. Instead, it would occupy CRK-II&#x27;s SH3 binding sites and act as a **dominant-negative antagonist**, blocking CRK-II&#x27;s ability to engage downstream effectors. This prevents CRK-II-dependent membrane ruffling and lamellipodia formation.<br><br>**Evaluating options:**<br>- A (active kinase): Incorrect — the domain lacks catalytic residues.<br>- C (scaffold activating CRK-II): Contradicts the antagonistic mechanism implied by the pseudokinase + SH3-binding structure.<br>- D (GTPase regulator): No GEF/GAP domain features are present.<br>- B matches perfectly: a pseudokinase that binds CRK-II via proline-rich regions and antagonizes its signaling.<br><br>2. answer: B</td>

3. The hint about membrane-related structure fits - the cilium has a membrane (axonemal membrane) and proteins can associate with it without having transmembrane helices

Option A (Mitochondrial matrix) - unlikely for a retinal protein
Option C (Nucleolus) - unlikely for a retinal protein
Option D (Endoplasmic reticulum) - while ER is a membrane structure, retinal proteins typically function in the photoreceptor, not ER

The answer is B. Cilium.
